## User · 2026-11-22T20:23:29.453Z

# Using Git Worktrees

## Overview

Ensure work happens in an isolated workspace. Prefer your platform's native worktree tools. Fall back to manual git worktrees only when no native tool is available.

**Core principle:** Detect existing isolation first. Then use native tools. Then fall back to git. Never fight the harness.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the using-git-worktrees skill to set up an isolated workspace."

## Step 0: Detect Existing Isolation

**Before creating anything, check if you are already in an isolated workspace.**

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

**Submodule guard:** `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` is also true inside git submodules. Before concluding "already in a worktree," verify you are not in a submodule:

```bash
# If this returns a path, you're in a submodule, not a worktree — treat as normal repo
git rev-parse --show-superproject-working-tree 2>/dev/null
```

**If `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` (and not a submodule):** You are already in a linked worktree. Skip to Step 2 (Project Setup). Do NOT create another worktree.

Report with branch state:
- On a branch: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` on branch `<name>`."
- Detached HEAD: "Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` (detached HEAD, externally managed). Branch creation needed at finish time."

**If `GIT_DIR == GIT_COMMON` (or in a submodule):** You are in a normal repo checkout.

Has the user already indicated their worktree preference in your instructions? If not, ask for consent before creating a worktree:

> "Would you like me to set up an isolated worktree? It protects your current branch from changes."

Honor any existing declared preference without asking. If the user declines consent, work in place and skip to Step 2.

## Step 1: Create Isolated Workspace

**You have two mechanisms. Try them in this order.**

### 1a. Native Worktree Tools (preferred)

The user has asked for an isolated workspace (Step 0 consent). Do you already have a way to create a worktree? It might be a tool with a name like `EnterWorktree`, `WorktreeCreate`, a `/worktree` command, or a `--worktree` flag. If you do, use it and skip to Step 2.

Native tools handle directory placement, branch creation, and cleanup automatically. Using `git worktree add` when you have a native tool creates phantom state your harness can't see or manage.

Only proceed to Step 1b if you have no native worktree tool available.

### 1b. Git Worktree Fallback

**Only use this if Step 1a does not apply** — you have no native worktree tool available. Create a worktree manually using git.

#### Directory Selection

Follow this priority order. Explicit user preference always beats observed filesystem state.

1. **Check your instructions for a declared worktree directory preference.** If the user has already specified one, use it without asking.

2. **Check for an existing project-local worktree directory:**
   ```bash
   ls -d .worktrees 2>/dev/null     # Preferred (hidden)
   ls -d worktrees 2>/dev/null      # Alternative
   ```
   If found, use it. If both exist, `.worktrees` wins.

3. **If there is no other guidance available**, default to `.worktrees/` at the project root.

#### Safety Verification (project-local directories only)

**MUST verify directory is ignored before creating worktree:**

```bash
git check-ignore -q .worktrees 2>/dev/null || git check-ignore -q worktrees 2>/dev/null
```

**If NOT ignored:** Add to .gitignore, commit the change, then proceed.

**Why critical:** Prevents accidentally committing worktree contents to repository.

#### Create the Worktree

```bash
# Determine path based on chosen location
path="$LOCATION/$BRANCH_NAME"

git worktree add "$path" -b "$BRANCH_NAME"
cd "$path"
```

**Sandbox fallback:** If `git worktree add` fails with a permission error (sandbox denial), tell the user the sandbox blocked worktree creation and you're working in the current directory instead. Then run setup and baseline tests in place.

## Step 2: Project Setup

Auto-detect and run appropriate setup:

```bash
# Node.js
if [ -f package.json ]; then npm install; fi

# Rust
if [ -f Cargo.toml ]; then cargo build; fi

# Python
if [ -f requirements.txt ]; then pip install -r requirements.txt; fi
if [ -f pyproject.toml ]; then poetry install; fi

# Go
if [ -f go.mod ]; then go mod download; fi
```

## Step 3: Verify Clean Baseline

Run tests to ensure workspace starts clean:

```bash
# Use project-appropriate command
npm test / cargo test / pytest / go test ./...
```

**If tests fail:** Report failures, ask whether to proceed or investigate.

**If tests pass:** Report ready.

### Report

```
Worktree ready at <full-path>
Tests passing (<N> tests, 0 failures)
Ready to implement <feature-name>
```

## Quick Reference

| Situation | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| Already in linked worktree | Skip creation (Step 0) |
| In a submodule | Treat as normal repo (Step 0 guard) |
| Native worktree tool available | Use it (Step 1a) |
| No native tool | Git worktree fallback (Step 1b) |
| `.worktrees/` exists | Use it (verify ignored) |
| `worktrees/` exists | Use it (verify ignored) |
| Both exist | Use `.worktrees/` |
| Neither exists | Check instruction file, then default `.worktrees/` |
| Directory not ignored | Add to .gitignore + commit |
| Permission error on create | Sandbox fallback, work in place |
| Tests fail during baseline | Report failures + ask |
| No package.json/Cargo.toml | Skip dependency install |

## Common Rationalizations

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "I'm obviously not in a worktree — no need to check" | Run Step 0. Harness-created isolation and submodules both fool eyeballing; the detection commands settle it. |
| "`git worktree add` is quicker than hunting for a native tool" | A native tool (e.g. `EnterWorktree`) owns placement, branching, and cleanup. Bypassing it is the #1 mistake — it creates phantom state your harness can't see or manage. |
| "The worktree directory is surely ignored already" | Run `git check-ignore`. An unignored worktree directory commits the whole tree into the repo. |
| "Any directory name works" | Explicit instructions beat an existing project-local directory, which beats the `.worktrees/` default. |
| "The workspace is fresh — baseline tests can wait" | A dirty baseline makes every later failure ambiguous. Run the tests now; proceeding past failures is your human partner's call. |

=== src/api/client.ts
// Reusable API client: every API call in the app goes through apiFetch.

/** How long a request may run before we give up (ms). */
export const REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000;

export type ApiErrorCode = "http" | "network" | "timeout" | "parse";

/**
 * The only error type apiFetch throws (besides a caller-initiated abort).
 * - http:    server answered with non-2xx; `status` and parsed `body` are set
 * - network: request never got an answer (server down, offline)
 * - timeout: no answer within the timeout
 * - parse:   2xx answer that is not valid JSON
 */
export class ApiError extends Error {
  readonly code: ApiErrorCode;
  readonly status: number; // 0 when there was no HTTP response
  readonly body: unknown; // parsed JSON error body, if the server sent one

  constructor(code: ApiErrorCode, message: string, status = 0, body?: unknown) {
    super(message);
    this.name = "ApiError";
    this.code = code;
    this.status = status;
    this.body = body;
  }
}

export type ApiOptions = Omit<RequestInit, "signal"> & {
  /** Abort from outside (e.g. React effect cleanup). */
  signal?: AbortSignal;
  /** Override the default timeout. */
  timeoutMs?: number;
};

15	 * - parse:   2xx answer that is not valid JSON
16	 */
17	export class ApiError extends Error {
18	  readonly code: ApiErrorCode;
19	  readonly status: number; // 0 when there was no HTTP response
20	  readonly body: unknown; // parsed JSON error body, if the server sent one
21	
22	  constructor(code: ApiErrorCode, message: string, status = 0, body?: unknown) {
23	    super(message);
24	    this.name = "ApiError";
25	    this.code = code;
26	    this.status = status;
27	    this.body = body;
28	  }
29	}
30	
31	export type ApiOptions = Omit<RequestInit, "signal"> & {
32	  /** Abort from outside (e.g. React effect cleanup). */
33	  signal?: AbortSignal;
34	  /** Override the default timeout. */
35	  timeoutMs?: number;
36	};
37	
38	/**
39	 * fetch + JSON parsing + typed errors + timeout.
40	 * Resolves with the parsed JSON body of a 2xx response, otherwise throws ApiError.

import { defineConfig } from "vite";
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react";

// Dev proxy: the browser calls /api/... on the Vite port, Vite forwards it to
// the local Go server (cmd/local) so there are no CORS issues in development.
// Everything local listens on 127.0.0.1 only (CLAUDE.md). The proxy target is 127.0.0.1, not "localhost":
// Node may resolve "localhost" to the IPv6 ::1, where cmd/local does not listen.
//
// The object form (not the string shorthand) on purpose (Task 14): the shorthand turns on `changeOrigin`,
// which rewrites the Host header to 127.0.0.1:8080. The API's CSRF check compares the browser's Origin
// (127.0.0.1:5173) with the host, so every POST (sign in, sign out...) would get 403. Here the Host header is
// kept and `xfwd` adds X-Forwarded-Host - the same header Vercel's proxy sends in production.
// `vite preview` uses the same proxy (preview.proxy defaults to server.proxy).
export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  server: {
    host: "127.0.0.1",
    proxy: {
      "/api": { target: "http://127.0.0.1:8080", xfwd: true },
    },
  },
  preview: {
    host: "127.0.0.1",
  },
});

// Tiny helper for component/hook tests (jsdom). The project has no Testing Library, so we render
// with React's own createRoot inside act().
import { act, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";

// Tell React that this environment is a test (enables act() and silences its warning).
(globalThis as { IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT?: boolean }).IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT = true;

/** Renders `ui` into a fresh <div> that is attached to the document (so focus works). */
export function render(ui: ReactNode) {
  const container = document.createElement("div");
  document.body.append(container);
  const root = createRoot(container);
  act(() => root.render(ui));
  return {
    container,
    /** Renders something else into the same root (same component = its state is kept, props change). */
    rerender(next: ReactNode) {
      act(() => root.render(next));
    },
    unmount() {
      act(() => root.unmount());
      container.remove();
    },
  };
}

# Design reference

The single source of truth for how every screen looks. Every UI task follows this file and uses the variables in
[`src/styles/tokens.css`](../src/styles/tokens.css). The base styles (reset, font, focus ring) are in
[`src/styles/global.css`](../src/styles/global.css).

The goal is the **look** of eneba.com (colors, type, spacing, shapes, layout) — not its brand. We do not copy the logo,
icons, illustrations or images. The wordmark is the text placeholder `APP_NAME` until the owner picks a name.

**Contents:** [1. How to use](#1-how-to-use) · [2. Tokens](#2-tokens) · [3. Layout](#3-layout) ·

// One QueryClient for the whole app = one cache. TanStack Query keeps every server response in it,
// so a page that was already visited can show its data immediately (no empty flash).
// All requests inside it still go through apiFetch (src/api/client.ts), never through a raw fetch.
import { QueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { ApiError } from "../api/client.ts";

/**
 * Retry rule: ask again ONCE, and only when asking again can help.
 * - network error / 5xx: the server may just be waking up (Neon, cold start) -> retry once.
 * - 4xx (404, 400...): the answer would be the same -> do not retry.
 * - timeout: the user already waited 15 s; retrying would double that -> do not retry.
 * - anything else (parse error, a bug in our code): retrying does not help.
 * `failureCount` is 0 after the first failure.
 */
export function shouldRetry(failureCount: number, error: unknown): boolean {
  if (failureCount >= 1) return false;
  if (!(error instanceof ApiError)) return false;
  if (error.code === "network") return true;
  return error.code === "http" && error.status >= 500;
}

export function createQueryClient(): QueryClient {
  return new QueryClient({
    defaultOptions: {
      queries: {
        // staleTime: for 30 s a cached answer counts as fresh. Going back to the Store (or a product)
        // shows the cached data instantly and does NOT refetch. After 30 s it is still shown at once,
        // and silently refreshed in the background. Game data and pric
… [truncated; original 3779 chars]

/* Sort radios in the drawer: see docs/DESIGN.md 4.6 (Eneba: 20px circle, 2px border; checked = yellow ring + 10px yellow dot; 14px text). */
.list {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column; /* no gap: every row is already a 40px tap target (Eneba: 35px from row to row) */
}

.row {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: var(--space-3);
  min-height: var(--hit-target-min); /* the drawer is only used on touch-sized screens */
  cursor: pointer;
  font-size: var(--font-size-sm);
  line-height: var(--line-height-tight);
}

/* The real <input> is the circle (appearance: none), so keyboard and screen readers get a real radio. */
.radio {
  appearance: none;
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  flex-shrink: 0;
  width: var(--checkbox-size);
  height: var(--checkbox-size);
  margin: 0;
  border: 2px solid var(--color-border-control);
  border-radius: var(--radius-round);
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: border-color var(--transition-fast);

/**
 * POST with an optional JSON body (auth, orders, checkout). The browser adds the Origin header itself;
 * the server checks it (CSRF, Task 14).

export function postJSON<T>(path: string, body?: unknown, options?: ApiOptions): Promise<T> {
  return apiFetch<T>(path, {
    ...options,
    method: "POST",
    headers: body === undefined ? undefined : { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body),
  });
}

// Typed functions for checkout and orders (Task 16a API contract, §4). Every call goes through apiFetch.
// The server is the source of truth: it re-prices the cart, reserves the keys and decides every status.
// The browser only sends what the user chose and shows what the server answered.

  CartChangedResponse,

/** POST /api/checkout/quote (no sign-in needed): today's price, stock and availability of each cart line. */
export function quoteCart(items: CheckoutItem[], options?: ApiOptions): Promise<QuoteResponse> {
  return postJSON<QuoteResponse>("/api/checkout/quote", { items }, options);
}

 * The cart is not touched: it stays in localStorage across sign-in and sign-out (owner decision).
 * Cached orders ARE dropped (Task 16b): they belong to the previous user and must never flash for the next one.
 */
export function useAuthMutations() {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const dropOrders = () => queryClient.removeQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.orders });
  const signedIn = (user: User) => {
    dropOrders();
    queryClient.setQueryData(queryKeys.currentUser, user);
  };

// The client side of idempotency (Task 16a K4): one "checkout attempt" = one idempotencyKey.
//
// The server promises: the same key from the same user returns the SAME order (no second order, no second
// reservation). So the browser must send the same key again when it repeats the same attempt - a double click,
// or "Checkout" pressed again after a network error (the first request may have created the order even though
// the answer never arrived). It must send a NEW key when the cart is different, because that is a new order.
//
// So the key is stored together with a fingerprint of the cart it belongs to:
//   same cart  -> same key;   cart changed -> new key;   order created -> key forgotten.
// sessionStorage (not localStorage): it survives a page reload but belongs to this one tab, and is gone when the tab closes.
import type { CheckoutItem } from "../../api/types.ts";

export const ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY = "checkout-attempt:v1";

type Storage = Pick<globalThis.Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem">;

interface StoredAttempt {
  key: string;
  cart: string;
}

// "Reserved for mm:ss" on the payment page, corrected for a wrong clock on the user's computer.
//
// The reservation ends at `reservedUntil`, a time on the SERVER's clock. If the user's clock is 5 minutes fast,
// "reservedUntil - Date.now()" would show 5 minutes too little (or "expired" while the keys are still reserved).
// So the server also sends `serverTime` ("now" on its clock). When that answer arrives we compute
//   offset = serverTime - (the browser's time at that moment)
// and from then on "server now" = Date.now() + offset. Only the DIFFERENCE between the two clocks matters, and it
// does not change while the page is open. (The few milliseconds the answer travelled are ignored.)

/** How far the server's clock is ahead of this browser's clock (negative = behind). */
export function clockOffsetMs(serverTime: string, receivedAtMs: number): number {
  const server = Date.parse(serverTime);
  return Number.isNaN(server) ? 0 : server - receivedAtMs;
}

/** Milliseconds left until `reservedUntil` (server clock), never below 0. */

// Small text helpers of the order pages.
import type { OrderStatus } from "../../api/types.ts";

/** The status badge text. Words, not only colours: the badge must be readable without seeing the colour. */
export const STATUS_LABELS: Record<OrderStatus, string> = {
  paid: "Paid",
  pending: "Pending payment",
  expired: "Expired",
  cancelled: "Cancelled",
};

/** A status the app does not know yet (a newer server) is shown as the server wrote it, never crashes. */
export function statusLabel(status: string): string {
  return STATUS_LABELS[status as OrderStatus] ?? status;
}

/**
 * "DEMO-AB12-CD34-EF56" → "DEMO-••••-••••-••••": the first group stays (it says what kind of key it is), every
 * other character is replaced, the dashes stay so the length and shape are visible. A key without dashes is hidden completely.
 * Masking is only about the SCREEN (someone looking over the shoulder, a screen share); the key is the user's own.
 */
export function maskKey(key: string): string {
  const parts = key.split("-");
  if (parts.length === 1) return "•".repeat(key.length);
  return [parts[0], ...parts.slice(1).map((part) => "•".repeat(part.length))].join("-");
}

/**
 * An order time ("2026-11-22T14:05:00Z") -> "22 Nov 2026, 14:05" in the user's OWN time zone (unlike a release date,
 * this is a moment, so local time is right). `timeZone` is only for tests. Anything unexpected is shown as sent.
 */
export function formatDateTime(iso: string, timeZone?: string): string {
  const date = new Date(iso);
  if (Number.isNaN(date.getTime())) return iso;
  return new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-IE", { dateStyle: "medium", timeStyle: "short", timeZone }).format(date);
}

// The per-line statuses of the cart page (notices + "no longer available"), kept outside React like the cart
// itself (cartStore.ts) and the toast (lib/toast.ts): a value + listeners, read with useSyncExternalStore.
// Outside React because the answer that changes them arrives inside a query function / mutation callback,

let statuses: LineStatuses = {};
const listeners = new Set<() => void>();

function set(next: LineStatuses) {
  statuses = next;
  for (const listener of listeners) listener();
}

function subscribe(listener: () => void) {
  listeners.add(listener);
  return () => listeners.delete(listener);
}

import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

/**
 * The current time (Date.now()), updated every `intervalMs`: the component re-renders on every tick.
 * Used by the payment page countdown. The interval is cleared when the component goes away.
 */
export function useNow(intervalMs = 1000): number {
  const [now, setNow] = useState(() => Date.now());
  useEffect(() => {
    const timer = setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), intervalMs);
    return () => clearInterval(timer);
  }, [intervalMs]);
  return now;
}
```

/* Quantity stepper of a cart line (DESIGN.md 3.6): "− 1 +", 16px semibold number, small icon buttons (Eneba: 85x25 in total). */
.stepper {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: var(--space-1);
}

.button {
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  width: var(--icon-size);
  height: var(--icon-size);
  padding: 0;
  border: 0;
  background: none;
  color: var(--color-text);
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: color var(--transition-fast);
}

.button:hover {
  color: var(--color-accent);
}

.button:disabled {
  opacity: 0.5;
  cursor: not-allowed;
  pointer-events: none;
}

.icon {
  width: var(--font-size-base);
  height: var(--font-size-base);
}

.value {
  min-width: var(--icon-size);
  font-size: var(--font-size-base);
  font-weight: var(--font-weight-medium);
  line-height: var(--line-height-tight);
  text-align: center;
}

/* Phones: 40px tap targets (DESIGN.md 5.2). */
@media (max-width: 600px) {
  .button {
    width: var(--hit-target-min);
    height: var(--hit-target-min);
  }
}

import { Copy, Eye, EyeOff } from "lucide-react";
import { useState } from "react";
import { VisuallyHidden } from "../../components/VisuallyHidden.tsx";
import { showToast } from "../../lib/toast.ts";

/**
 * Copies `text` to the clipboard and says so in a toast. navigator.clipboard works only on https or localhost and may
 * be refused by the browser; then the user is told how to copy by hand instead of nothing happening.
 */
export async function copyKey(text: string) {
  try {
    await navigator.clipboard.writeText(text);
    showToast({ kind: "success", message: "Copied" });
  } catch {
    showToast({ kind: "info", message: "Could not copy. Reveal the key and copy it by hand." });
  }
}

/**
 * "#0B9F2C1E": the first 8 characters of the order's UUID, to tell orders apart on screen. Only a label - the full id is
 * in the URL, and the server finds orders by the full id.
 */
export function shortOrderId(id: string): string {
  return `#${id.slice(0, 8).toUpperCase()}`;
}

```
import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { orderListQuery } from "../api/queries.ts";
import { ErrorState } from "../components/ErrorState.tsx";
import { OrderList } from "../features/orders/OrderList.tsx";
import { OrderLoading } from "../features/orders/OrderLoading.tsx";
import { useDocumentTitle } from "../lib/useDocumentTitle.ts";

/** /orders (signed in only, RequireAuth): the user's orders, newest first. */
export function OrdersPage() {
  useDocumentTitle("Orders");
  const query = useQuery(orderListQuery());

  if (query.isPending) return <OrderLoading />;
  if (query.isError) {
    return <ErrorState message="Could not load your orders." onRetry={() => void query.refetch()} retrying={query.isFetching} />;
  }
  return <OrderList orders={query.data} />;
}
```

import { Skeleton } from "../../components/Skeleton.tsx";
import { VisuallyHidden } from "../../components/VisuallyHidden.tsx";
import layout from "../checkout/CheckoutLayout.module.css";

/**
 * Placeholder of the order pages while they load (DESIGN.md 4.10): the panel with three bars, no "Loading…" on screen.
 * `label` is what screen readers hear ("Loading the order…").
 */
export function OrderLoading({ label }: { label: string }) {
  return (
    <div className={layout.page} aria-busy="true">
      <div className={layout.panel}>
        <VisuallyHidden>{label}</VisuallyHidden>
        <Skeleton />
        <Skeleton />
        <Skeleton />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

```
// POST helper shared by auth.ts and orders.ts (moved out of auth.ts in Task 16b).
// It is a separate module on purpose: tests replace `apiFetch` (vi.mock of client.ts), and only calls that go
// through the module's EXPORT see the replacement - a helper inside client.ts would call the real one.
import { apiFetch, type ApiOptions } from "./client.ts";

/**
 * POST with an optional JSON body. The browser adds the Origin header itself; the server checks it (CSRF, Task 14).
 */
export function postJSON<T>(path: string, body?: unknown, options?: ApiOptions): Promise<T> {
  return apiFetch<T>(path, {
    ...options,
    method: "POST",
    headers: body === undefined ? undefined : { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body),
  });
}
```

const settle = async () => {
  for (let tick = 0; tick < 5; tick++) await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 0));
};
const flush = () => act(settle);

async function mount(url = "/cart") {
  const router = createMemoryRouter(routes, { initialEntries: [url] });
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  const view = render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <RouterProvider router={router} />
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
  cleanup = view.unmount;
  await flush();
  return { router };
}

// Test helper (used only by *.test.tsx) for the checkout and order pages: the REAL app routes in a memory router,
// with apiFetch answered by the in-memory fake server (fakeAuthServer.ts). Every module-level store the pages use
// (cart, line statuses, attempt key, toast) is reset between tests.
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { act } from "react";
import { createMemoryRouter, RouterProvider } from "react-router";
import { routes } from "../../app/routes.tsx";
import { render } from "../../test-utils.tsx";
import { cartStore } from "../cart/cartStore.ts";
import { checkoutAttempt } from "./attemptKey.ts";
import { lineStatusStore } from "./lineStatusStore.ts";

export type TestRouter = ReturnType<typeof createMemoryRouter>;

let cleanup: (() => void) | undefined;

export function resetCheckoutState() {
  cleanup?.();
  cleanup = undefined;
  cartStore.clear();
  lineStatusStore.reset();
  checkoutAttempt.clear();
  window.scrollTo = (() => {}) as never; // jsdom has no scrolling; the layout's ScrollRestoration calls it
}

export function unmountApp() {
  cleanup?.();
  cleanup = undefined;
}

// Typed functions for the checkout endpoints (Task 16a, pkg/orders). Every call goes through apiFetch.
// No UI here: the cart and checkout screens (Task 16b) call these from TanStack Query.
import { ApiError, apiFetch, type ApiOptions } from "./client.ts";
import type {
  CartChangedBody,
  CartLineInput,
  CheckoutLine,
  Order,
  OrderDetailResponse,
  OrderResponse,
  OrdersResponse,
  OrderSummary,
  PaymentOutcome,
  Quote,
} from "./types.ts";

/** POST with a JSON body. The browser adds the Origin header itself; the server checks it (CSRF). */
function postJSON<T>(path: string, body?: unknown, options?: ApiOptions): Promise<T> {
  return apiFetch<T>(path, {
    ...options,
    method: "POST",
    headers: body === undefined ? undefined : { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body),
  });
}

/** POST /api/checkout/quote (no sign-in): re-prices the cart; each line says what can really be bought. */
export function quoteCart(items: CartLineInput[], options?: ApiOptions): Promise<Quote> {
  return postJSON<Quote>("/api/checkout/quote", { items }, options);
}

**Tool: Bash**

```
$ node -e '
const hex=h=>[1,3,5].map(i=>parseInt(h.slice(i,i+2),16));
const over=(fg,a,bg)=>fg.map((c,i)=>c*a+bg[i]*(1-a));
const L=c=>{const [r,g,b]=c.map(v=>{v/=255;return v<=0.03928?v/12.92:((v+0.055)/1.055)**2.4});return 0.2126*r+0.7152*g+0.0722*b};
const cr=(a,b)=>{const [x,y]=[L(a),L(b)].sort((p,q)=>q-p);return ((x+0.05)/(y+0.05)).toFixed(2)};
const deep=hex("#351183"); const line=over([0,0,0],0.12,deep); const inset=over([0,0,0],0.2,line);
const white=[255,255,255]; const muted=over(white,0.7,line); const mutedDeep=over(white,0.7,deep);
console.log("line bg", line.map(Math.round));
for (const [n,c] of [["warning","#f0a613"],["error","#f0a3b8"],["accent","#fad318"],["secondary","#23c299"],["white","#ffffff"]]) console.log(n,"on line",cr(hex(c),line),"on deep",cr(hex(c),deep));
console.log("muted on line",cr(muted,line),"muted on deep",cr(mutedDeep,deep));
console.log("white on key inset (over line)",cr(white,inset), "accent on line", cr(hex("#fad318"),line));
console.log("error-border on deep (non-text)",cr(hex("#e76a8c"),deep));
console.log("dimmed white (0.8) on line", cr(over(white,0.8,line),line), "dimmed muted", cr(over(muted,0.8,line),line));
'

### 2.4 Breakpoints and layout tokens

664	# Task 14 – paskyros: registracija, prisijungimas, atsijungimas, demo vartotojas, apsaugoti puslapiai
665	
666	## Duomenų bazė: `users`, `sessions`, `login_failures` (`migrations/00004_auth.sql`)
667	- **Ką daro:** `users` – paskyros (`email citext UNIQUE`, `password_hash`, `is_demo`). `sessions` – prisijungimai: raktas yra **tokeno SHA-256** (`token_hash bytea PRIMARY KEY`), `user_id` → `users` su `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `expires_at`. `login_failures` – nesėkmingų bandymų skaitliukas pagal įvestą el. paštą.
668	- **Kodėl taip:** `citext` – „[REDACTED]" ir „[REDACTED]" yra ta pati paskyra, o `UNIQUE` tai užtikrina net kai du žmonės registruojasi vienu metu (sprendžia DB, ne ankstesnis `SELECT`). CHECK `(password_hash IS NULL) = is_demo`: paprasta paskyra visada turi slaptažodį, demo – niekada. `CASCADE` tik sesijoms: tai laikina prisijungimo būsena, ne istorija (visur kitur `RESTRICT`).
669	- **Gali paklausti:** „Kodėl `login_failures.email` nėra FK į `users`?" – skaičiuojami ir **neegzistuojantys** el. paštai; kitaip 429 vs 401 atskleistų, kurie el. paštai užregistruoti.
670

The API is fine; my selector was wrong (the card button's name comes from its text, not `aria-label`). Fixing the script.

old:
```
  const added = await page.$$eval("button[aria-label^='Add to cart:']", (bs) => {
    const names = [];
    for (const b of bs.slice(0, 2)) {
      b.click();
      names.push(b.getAttribute("aria-label").replace("Add to cart: ", ""));
    }
    return names;
  });
```
new:
```
  const added = await page.$$eval("main button", (all) => {
    const bs = all.filter((b) => b.textContent.startsWith("Add to cart: "));
    const names = [];
    for (const b of bs.slice(0, 2)) {
      b.click();
      names.push(b.textContent.replace("Add to cart: ", ""));
    }
    return names;
  });
```

Task 16b is built, tested and committed locally on `task/16b-checkout-ui` (in the `../ENEBA-homework-16b` worktree). There is no PR yet: it needs 16a merged first.

- **What works:**
  - The cart re-checks prices and stock with the server on every visit and shows the three notices.
  - Checkout makes you sign in and reuses the same idempotency key for retries and double clicks.
  - The payment page has a countdown based on the server's clock and a simulated Success / Decline box with no card fields.
  - Orders list and order page: keys are masked, with Reveal and Copy.
- **Checks:** all CLAUDE.md checks pass, including 530/530 frontend tests. In a real browser, 49/49 checks passed against a local mock (including the 15-minute reservation running out, shortened to 5 s in the mock). 22/22 passed against 16a's real server on the Neon `dev` database. axe found 0 violations on every page.
- **Eneba comparison:** I measured Eneba's real cart page by adding items as a user would. The table of remaining differences is in the report.
- **Reviewer:** the `task-reviewer` found missing tests for the API error helpers and a screen-reader issue on the "items changed" line. Both are fixed; the rest is answered in section 7 of the report.
- **Not pushed yet, on purpose:** rebasing a branch that is already pushed would need a force-push, which CLAUDE.md forbids. So I'll push only after the rebase on 16a.

**Next step for you:** merge 16a (PR #17) after its production-migration step, then tell me. I'll rebase onto `main` and switch the UI to 16a's type names (the JSON is identical). Then I'll re-run the real-API check, push, open the PR and wait for CI, and remove the worktree.

Open questions are in section 6 of the report, among them: should Eneba's desktop cart popup be added? Should a second checkout of the same cart reopen the existing pending order instead of creating a new one?

origin/main:README.md:207:- **At most 3 unpaid orders per user (R10):** a user who already holds 3 *pending, not yet expired* orders gets `409 "Too many unpaid orders – pay or cancel one first"` on the next `POST /api/orders`. Paying, cancelling or letting one expire frees a place. It stops a script from reserving every key of an offer, 15 minutes at a time. The check runs **inside the create transaction** after locking the user's row (`SELECT … FOR NO KEY UPDATE`), so eight parallel creates of one user produce exactly three orders. A *retry* of an order that already exists (same idempotency key) is still answered with that order, also at the cap. There is no per-IP rate limit (see the shortcuts).

export function isPaymentDeclined(err: unknown): boolean {
  return err instanceof ApiError && err.code === "http" && err.status === 402;
}

/** True for the 409 "Reservation expired" answer of pay (other 409s, e.g. "not payable", are false). */
export function isReservationExpired(err: unknown): boolean {
  if (!(err instanceof ApiError) || err.code !== "http" || err.status !== 409) return false;
  return (err.body as { error?: unknown } | undefined)?.error === "Reservation expired";
}

/** The text of the 409 answer of POST /api/orders when the user already holds 3 open unpaid orders. */

export function isTooManyPendingOrders(err: unknown): boolean {
  if (!(err instanceof ApiError) || err.code !== "http" || err.status !== 409) return false;
  return (err.body as { error?: unknown } | undefined)?.error === TOO_MANY_PENDING_MESSAGE;
}

// ---- Task 16b: the UI's error readers ----

/** True when `err` is an HTTP answer with exactly this status (404 → "Page not found", 401 → sign in…). */
export function isHttpStatus(err: unknown, status: number): boolean {
  return err instanceof ApiError && err.code === "http" && err.status === status;
}

/** Statuses whose `{"error": "..."}` text is written for the user (validation, declined, not payable / too many unpaid). */

1	// The client side of idempotency (Task 16a K4): one "checkout attempt" = one idempotencyKey.
2	//
3	// The server promises: the same key from the same user returns the SAME order (no second order, no second
4	// reservation). So the browser must send the same key again when it repeats the same attempt - a double click,
5	// or "Checkout" pressed again after a network error (the first request may have created the order even though
6	// the answer never arrived). It must send a NEW key when the cart is different, because that is a new order.
7	//
8	// So the key is stored together with a fingerprint of the cart it belongs to:
9	//   same cart  -> same key;   cart changed -> new key;   order created -> key forgotten.
10	// sessionStorage (not localStorage): it survives a page reload but belongs to this one tab, and is gone when the tab closes.
11	import type { CartLineInput } from "../../api/types.ts";
12

## Summary
The checkout UI on top of the 16a API. **`/cart`** (Eneba's cart layout, measured on eneba.com) re-quotes the cart on load and on every change, and shows per-line notices: price updated, quantity reduced, no longer available. **Checkout** requires sign-in. It sends one idempotency key per attempt, kept in sessionStorage, and applies `409 cart_changed`. It also handles the new `409 "Too many unpaid orders"`: the message plus a link to `/orders`.
**`/checkout/:orderId`** has a countdown on the server's clock and a simulated Success / Decline payment with **no card fields**, plus decline, expired and cancel states. **`/orders`** and **`/orders/:id`** show status badges and masked keys with Reveal / Copy; an unknown order gives 404.
Rebased on 16a: 16a's `src/api/types.ts` / `orders.ts` were kept, and the UI uses their names. **No migration, no Go changes.**

Task 16b is rebased on 16a, checked against the real API on the local `dev` database, and open as PR #18. CI is green.

- **Rebase:** where the two branches clashed I kept 16a's `src/api/types.ts`, `orders.ts` and `orders.test.ts`, and the UI now uses 16a's names. I added only what 16a didn't have: two error helpers with their tests, and one shared `postJSON` instead of two copies. The Lithuanian notes keep both blocks, 16a first. No Go changes and no migration.
- **"Too many unpaid orders":** Checkout shows the server's message with a "View your orders" link to `/orders`. The cart stays as it is and the idempotency key is kept, so after you pay or cancel an order, the next Checkout repeats the same attempt.
- **Real API:**
  - 29/29 browser checks pass on the full flow: Store → cart notices → demo sign-in → Checkout → decline → success → keys (reveal / copy) → orders list.
  - It includes the cap: 3 pending orders, then the 4th Checkout shows the message, the link opens `/orders`, and after cancelling one, Checkout works again.
  - The test orders were cancelled afterwards, so their keys on `dev` are free again. One earlier, interrupted run left a single pending order on a throwaway demo user; it frees itself within 15 minutes.
- **Mock:** 49/49 again after the rebase. This is where expiry and the "cart changed" case are checked, since they need control over time and prices.
- **Checks:** all CLAUDE.md checks pass, including 541/541 frontend tests; axe found 0 violations.
- **CI on PR #18:** `go`, `migrations` and `frontend` all pass on the final commit, and the Vercel Preview deployed.
- **Worktree:** removed. The branch is pushed and unchanged.

Adding the known simplification (and the two other decided UI points) to the README's checkout shortcuts.

**Tool: Edit**

`c:\PROJEKTAI\ENEBA\ENEBA-homework-16b\README.md`

old:
```
- expiry is lazy: an expired pending order stays `pending` in the table until someone pays, cancels or reads it (reading shows `expired` but does not write).
```
new:
```
- expiry is lazy: an expired pending order stays `pending` in the table until someone pays, cancels or reads it (reading shows `expired` but does not write);
- **a second Checkout of the same cart creates a second pending order** (Task 16b, known simplification): the browser forgets the idempotency key once an
  order exists, so going Back from the payment page and pressing Checkout again reserves keys again, while the first order keeps its keys until it is paid,
  cancelled or expires (15 minutes). The cap of 3 unpaid orders per user limits this. A real shop would reopen the existing pending order when the cart is unchanged;
- the cart is a page only (`/cart`; no header popup as on Eneba), and "Cancel order" has no confirmation step (nothing is lost: the cart stays).
